Lemma 3.25
.

If
d(r_{j},c_{j})=d(r_{j+1},c_{j+1})
, then
\mathsf{path}_{j+1}
is weakly above
\mathsf{path}_{j}
.

Proof.

By the definition of crowding order,
d(r_{j},c_{j})=d(r_{j+1},c_{j+1})
implies
c_{j}>c_{j+1}
. Set
z_{j}:=c_{j}-c_{j+1}
. Then we have

c_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}=c_{j}-z_{j}-s=c_{j+1}-s=c_{j+1}^{(s)}
for
0\leqslant s\leqslant\alpha_{j+1}
. We need to show that

r_{j+1}^{(s)}\geqslant r_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
for
0\leqslant s\leqslant\alpha_{j+1}
.
Computing
T^{(s)}_{j-1}
from
T^{(s-1)}_{j-1}
for
1\leqslant s\leqslant\alpha_{j}
,
we denote
b
and
q
in Step
1
and Step
2
of
Definition
3.19
by
b_{j}^{(s)}
and
q_{j}^{(s)}
.

Since
(r_{j+1},c_{j+1})
is a corner cell in
T^{(z_{j})}_{j-1}
, we have

r^{(0)}_{j+1}\geqslant r^{(z_{j})}_{j}
.
We prove that, for
1\leqslant s\leqslant\alpha_{j+1}
, we have that

q_{j+1}^{(s)}\geqslant q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
, which implies

b_{j+1}^{(s)}\geqslant b_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
and thus
r_{j+1}^{(s)}\geqslant r_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
.

We prove
q_{j+1}^{(s)}\geqslant q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
by induction on
s
. First we check the case
k=1
. If
r^{(0)}_{j+1}>r_{j}^{(z_{j})}
, then
it is obvious that
q_{j+1}^{(1)}>q_{j}^{(z_{j}+1)}
. Otherwise if
r^{(0)}_{j+1}=r_{j}^{(z_{j})}
, we consider the following cases.

q_{j}^{(z_{j})}
is the only element in
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r_{j+1},c_{j+1})
.
Let
x=\mathsf{H}_{T^{(z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r_{j+1},c_{j+1})
,
y=\max(\mathsf{L}_{T^{(z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r_{j+1},c_{j+1}))
and

y^{\prime}=\max\{z\in\mathsf{L}^{+}_{T^{(z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r_{j+1},c_{j+1})\mid z\leqslant q_{j}^{(z_{j})}\}
.
See Figure
5
for illustration.

Case (1):
If
r_{j}^{(z_{j}+1)}=r_{j}^{(z_{j})}
, then
q_{j}^{(z_{j}+1)}=x
. If
r_{j+1}^{(1)}=r_{j+1}^{(0)}
, then
q_{j+1}^{(1)}=q_{j}^{(z_{j})}
.
If
r_{j+1}^{(1)}\neq r_{j+1}^{(0)}
, then
q_{j+1}^{(1)}
equals
y
when
y>y^{\prime}
and
q_{j}^{(z_{j})}
when
y=y^{\prime}
. In both cases

q_{j+1}^{(1)}\geqslant x=q_{j}^{(z_{j}+1)}
.

{{\begin{array}[lb]{*{1}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}y\\
-\\
y^{\prime}\\
\ast\\
x\quad q_{j}^{(z_{j})}\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}}\qquad{\begin{array}[lb]{*{1}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}y\\
-\\
q_{j}^{(z_{j})}\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}}\qquad{\begin{array}[lb]{*{1}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}y\\
-\\
q_{j}^{(z_{j})}\\
\ast\\
x\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}}}

Figure 5.
Cell
(r^{(0)}_{j+1},c^{(0)}_{j+1})=(r^{(z_{j})}_{j},c^{(z_{j})}_{j})
in
T^{(z_{j})}_{j-1}
(left);

in
T^{(0)}_{j}
, case(1) (middle),   case(2) (right).

Case (2):
If
r_{j}^{(z_{j}+1)}\neq r_{j}^{(z_{j})}
, then
q_{j}^{(z_{j}+1)}=y^{\prime}
.
In this case we have
\mathsf{H}_{T^{(z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r_{j+1}+1,c_{j+1}-1)\leqslant y^{\prime}\leqslant y
.
Since
\mathsf{H}_{T^{(0)}_{j}}(r_{j+1}+1,c_{j+1}-1)
is smaller or equal to
y^{\prime}
, we
have that
r_{j+1}^{(1)}\neq r_{j+1}^{(0)}
. Therefore
q_{j+1}^{(1)}
equals

y
when
y>y^{\prime}
and
q_{j}^{(z_{j})}
when
y=y^{\prime}
. In this
case
q_{j+1}^{(1)}\geqslant y^{\prime}=q_{j}^{(z_{j}+1)}
.

Now we have proved the base case
s=1
. Next, suppose it holds for some
s\geqslant 1
that
q_{j+1}^{(s)}\geqslant q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}

and
r_{j+1}^{(s)}\geqslant r_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
. The statement is similar to the argument of the base case. If
r_{j+1}^{(s)}>r_{j}^{(z_{j}+s)}
, it
is obvious that
q_{j+1}^{(s+1)}>q_{j}^{(s+1+z_{j})}
and thus
r_{j+1}^{(s+1)}\geqslant r_{j}^{(s+1+z_{j})}
. If
r_{j+1}^{(s)}=r_{j}^{(z_{j}+s)}
,
we discuss the following cases.
q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
is the only element in
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s+z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r^{(s+z_{j})}_{j},c^{(s+z_{j})}_{j})
.
Let
x=\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s+z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r^{(s+z_{j})}_{j},c^{(s+z_{j})}_{j})
,

y=\max(\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s+z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r^{(s+z_{j})}_{j},c^{(s+z_{j})}_{j}))
and
y^{\prime}=\max\{z\in\mathsf{L}^{+}_{T^{(s+z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r^{(s+z_{j})}_{j},c^{(s+z_{j})}_{j})\mid z\leqslant q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}\}
.
See Figure
6
for illustration.

Case (1):
If
r_{j}^{(s+1+z_{j})}=r_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
, then
q_{j}^{(s+1+z_{j})}=x
. If
r_{j+1}^{(s+1)}=r_{j+1}^{(s)}
, then
q_{j+1}^{(s+1)}=q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}\geqslant x
.
If
r_{j+1}^{(s+1)}\neq r_{j+1}^{(s)}
, then
q_{j+1}^{(s+1)}=\max\{z\in\mathsf{L}^{+}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r_{j+1}^{(s)},c_{j+1}^{(s)})\mid z\leqslant q_{j+1}^{(s)}\}\geqslant q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}\geqslant x
.
So in either case we have
q_{j+1}^{(s+1)}\geqslant q_{j}^{(s+1+z_{j})}
.

{{\begin{array}[lb]{*{1}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}y\\
-\\
y^{\prime}\\
\ast\\
x\quad q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}}\qquad{\begin{array}[lb]{*{1}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}y\\
-\\
q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}\quad q_{j+1}^{(s)}\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}}\qquad{\begin{array}[lb]{*{1}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}y\\
-\\
q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}\\
\ast\\
x\quad q_{j+1}^{(s)}\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}}}

Figure 6.
Cell
(r^{(s)}_{j+1},c^{(s)}_{j+1})=(r^{(s+z_{j})}_{j},c^{(s+z_{j})}_{j})
in
T^{(s+z_{j})}_{j-1}
(left);

in
T^{(s)}_{j}
, case(1) (middle),  case(2) (right).

Case (2):
If
r_{j}^{(s+1+z_{j})}\neq r_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
, then

q_{j}^{(s+1+z_{j})}=y^{\prime}
. In this case we have

\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s+z_{j})}_{j-1}}(r_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}+1,c_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}-1)\leqslant y^{\prime}\leqslant q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
.
Since
\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r^{(s)}_{j+1}+1,c^{(s)}_{j+1}-1)
is smaller or equal
to
q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
, we have that

r^{(s+1)}_{j+1}\neq r_{j+1}^{(s)}
.
Therefore
q_{j+1}^{(s+1)}=\max\{z\in\mathsf{L}^{+}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r^{(s)}_{j+1},c^{(s)}_{j+1})\mid z\leqslant q^{(s)}_{j+1}\}
.
By induction we
have
q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}\leqslant q_{j+1}^{(s)}
, thus
q_{j+1}^{(s+1)}\geqslant q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}\geqslant y^{\prime}=q_{j}^{(s+1+z_{j})}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.26
.

With the notations as above, let
0\leqslant j\leqslant e-1
,
0\leqslant s<\alpha_{j+1}

and
\mathcal{C}_{b}([T^{(s)}_{j},(r,c)])=[\,T^{(s+1)}_{j},(r^{\prime},c-1)]
for some

r,c,r^{\prime}
. Then in
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
,
column
c-1
is the rightmost column with nonzero arm excess and
(r^{\prime},c-1)
is the topmost cell in column
c-1
with nonzero arm excess.

Proof.

In any
\mathsf{path}_{j}
, consider the arm excess of its columns. Those with column index
c
such that
d(r_{j},c_{j})<c<c_{j}
started
with arm excess
0
, then changed to arm excess
1
when the insertion path
passed through that column, and immediately decreased to
0
.

Thus the
q_{j}^{(s)}
that is being moved to cell
(r^{\prime},c-1)
is always at the rightmost column containing nonzero arm excess.
When
c-1>d(r_{j},c_{j})
, the arm excess of the column
c-1
is exactly
1
,
(r^{\prime},c-1)
is also the topmost cell containing an arm.
For
c-1=d(r_{j},c_{j})
, the path
\mathsf{path}_{j}
has reached its destination. At that point, any column to the right of
d(r_{j},c_{j})
has
0

arm excess. It follows from Lemma
3.25
that the cell

(r_{j}^{(\alpha_{j})},c_{j}^{(\alpha_{j})})
is also the topmost cell containing an
arm.
∎

Proposition 3.27
.

The tableau
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
is a semistandard hook-valued tableau for all

0\leqslant j\leqslant e-1
and for all
0\leqslant s<\alpha_{j+1}
.

Proof.

We only need to check that the
q
in Step
2
of
Definition
3.19
is greater or equal to the hook entry and arm of the
cell
q
is to be inserted into.
When
q
is the only arm element, it is obvious that
q
is greater or equal to
the hook entry.

The case when
q
is not the only arm element can only happen when we reach the
destination column of the path.
By the proof of Lemma
3.25
, we have that for
q_{j+1}^{(s)}\geqslant q_{j}^{(s+z_{j})}
for
s\geqslant 1
and for
j
such
that
d(r_{j},c_{j})=d(r_{j+1},c_{j+1})
. Hence the statement follows by setting
k=\alpha_{j+1}
.
∎

Before we define the “inverse” of the uncrowding map
\mathcal{U}:\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)\to\sqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\times\hat{\mathcal{F}}(\mu/\lambda)
, we need to restrict our domain to a subset
\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}
of

\sqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\times\hat{\mathcal{F}}(\mu/\lambda)
, as the image of
\mathcal{U}
is not all of
\sqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\times\hat{\mathcal{F}}(\mu/\lambda)
. We define:

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}(\mu):=

\displaystyle\{(S,F)\in\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\times\mathcal{\hat{F}}(\mu/\lambda)\,\mid\,\mathsf{weight}(T^{(s)}_{j})=\mathsf{weight}(S),\forall\,0\leqslant j\leqslant e-1,\forall\,0\leqslant s\leqslant\alpha_{j+1}\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}:=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}(\mu).

Remark 3.28
.

From the perspective of the uncrowding map, the set-valued tableau
S
in Example
3.20
cannot be obtained from a
shape
(1,1)
hook-valued tableau via the uncrowding map as explained in Remark
3.21
. We say the cell
(1,2)
in
S

practices social distancing
. In this case,

\left(\,{{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}3\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}2\\
1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}3\\
2\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}}\;,\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.40881pt\vbox{\kern 0.2044pt\hbox{\kern 0.40881pt\hbox{\pagecolor{gray}\vbox to16.76434pt{\vss\hbox to16.35553pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-16.76434pt\vrule height=16.76434pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 16.35553pt\vrule height=16.76434pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-16.76434pt\hrule width=17.17316pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 16.35553pt\hrule width=17.17316pt,height=0.40881pt}\cr\lower 0.40881pt\vbox{\kern 0.2044pt\hbox{\kern 0.40881pt\hbox{\pagecolor{gray}\vbox to16.76434pt{\vss\hbox to16.35553pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-16.76434pt\vrule height=16.76434pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 16.35553pt\vrule height=16.76434pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-16.76434pt\hrule width=17.17316pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 16.35553pt\hrule width=17.17316pt,height=0.40881pt}&\lower 0.40881pt\vbox{\kern 0.2044pt\hbox{\kern 0.40881pt\vbox to16.76434pt{\vss\hbox to16.35553pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss}\kern-16.76434pt\vrule height=16.76434pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 16.35553pt\vrule height=16.76434pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-16.76434pt\hrule width=17.17316pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 16.35553pt\hrule width=17.17316pt,height=0.40881pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}\,\right)\notin\mathsf{K}_{(1,1)}.

The
(S,F)
in Example
3.24
is in
\mathsf{K}_{(3,2,1)}(5,3,2)
.

Definition 3.29
.

We can now define the
crowding map

\mathcal{C}
for any partition
\lambda
as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\colon\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)

\displaystyle(S,F)

\displaystyle\mapsto T^{(0)}_{e}.

Proposition 3.30
.

The image of the uncrowding map
\mathcal{U}:\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)\to\sqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\times\hat{\mathcal{F}}(\mu/\lambda)
is a subset of
\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}
. Moreover, we have

\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{U}=\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)}
.

Proof.

We show that if
\tilde{h}=\mathcal{V}_{b}(h)
, where
h\in\mathsf{HVT}
,
\mathcal{V}_{b}
is as defined in Definition
3.2

and
\tilde{h}
is obtained by moving some letter(s) from the cell
(r,c)
to
(\tilde{r},c+1)
(potentially adding a box), then

\mathcal{C}_{b}([\tilde{h},(\tilde{r},c+1)])=[h^{\prime},(r^{\prime},c)]
satisfies
[h^{\prime},(r^{\prime},c)]=[h,(r,c)]
.

We follow the notation used in Definitions
3.2
and
3.19
. Thus
a=\max(\mathsf{A}_{h}(r,c))
. We have
that
\mathsf{H}_{h}(\tilde{r},c)\leqslant a
. If cell
(r+1,c)
is in
h
, then
\mathsf{H}_{h}(r+1,c)>a
.

Case (1):

\tilde{r}\neq r
.

Case (1A):
If cell
(\tilde{r},c+1)
is not in
h
, then
h^{\prime}
is obtained by adding cell
(\tilde{r},c+1)
and moving
a
from

\mathsf{A}_{h}(r,c)
to
\mathsf{H}_{h}(\tilde{r},c+1)
.
Under the action of
\mathcal{C}_{b}
, by
Step
1
,
b=a
and
r^{\prime}=r
.
\mathcal{C}_{b}
appends
a
to

\mathsf{A}_{\tilde{h}}(r,c)
and removes cell
(\tilde{r},c+1)
, which recovers
h
.

\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\

\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{V}_{b}}{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\

\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\

-\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\

--\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\

(\tilde{r},c+1)

. Right: case (1B):

.

Case (1B):
If cell
(\tilde{r},c+1)
is in

, then
k\in\mathsf{L}^{+}_{h}(\tilde{r},c+1)
is the smallest number that is greater than or
equal to
a
in column

.
h^{\prime}
is obtained by removing
a
from
\mathsf{A}_{h}(r,c)
, replacing
k
with
a
, and attaching
k
to

\mathsf{A}_{h}(\tilde{r},c+1)
.
Under the action of
\mathcal{C}_{b}
, by Step
1
, we can see that

m=k
,
b=a
and
r^{\prime}=r
. By Step
3(b)i
,

q=b=a
, and
a
is appended to
\mathsf{A}_{\tilde{h}}(r,c)
and
q=a
in
\mathsf{L}_{\tilde{h}}(\tilde{r},c+1)
is replaced with
m=k
. In the
end,
m
is removed from
\mathsf{A}_{\tilde{h}}(\tilde{r},c+1)
. We recover
h
.

Case (2):

\tilde{r}=r
. Let
\ell=\max(\mathsf{L}^{+}_{h}(r,c))
.

Case (2A):
If cell
(r,c+1)
is not in
h
,
\mathcal{V}_{b}
adds cell

(r,c+1)
, removes the part of
\mathsf{L}_{h}(r,c)
that is greater than
a
to

\mathsf{L}_{h}(r,c+1)
and moves
a
from
\mathsf{A}_{h}(r,c)
to
\mathsf{H}_{h}(r,c+1)
.
Under the action of
\mathcal{C}_{b}
, by Step
1
,
m=a
and

b=\ell
. Thus
r^{\prime}=r
. By Step
3(a)ii
, we move
\mathsf{L}_{\tilde{h}}(r,c+1)
into
\mathsf{L}_{\tilde{h}}(r,c)
and we recover
h
.

.

to

.

,

and

. Then

and

. By Step

, we move the set

from
\mathsf{L}_{\tilde{h}}(r,c+1)

into
\mathsf{L}_{\tilde{h}}(r,c)
, which is the set that was moved from cell
(r,c)

by
\mathcal{V}_{b}
. Removing
k
from
\mathsf{A}_{\tilde{h}}(r,c+1)
and setting

\mathsf{H}_{\tilde{h}}(r,c+1)=k
, we recover
h
.

Now we have proven
\mathcal{C}_{b}([\tilde{h},(\tilde{r},c+1)])=[h^{\prime},(r^{\prime},c)]=[h,(r,c)]
. It follows that for any
(S,F)=\mathcal{U}(h)
, we
have that
T^{(s)}_{j}
is semistandard and has the same weight as
S
for all

0\leqslant j\leqslant e-1
, for all
0\leqslant s\leqslant\alpha_{j+1}
. Thus

\mathsf{image}(\mathcal{U})\subset\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}
and
\mathcal{C}\circ\mathcal{U}=\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)}
.
∎

Proposition 3.31
.

\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}
is a subset of the image of

\mathcal{U}:\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)\to\sqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\times\hat{\mathcal{F}}(\mu/\lambda)
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{U}\circ\mathcal{C}=\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}}
.

Proof.

Let
(S,F)\in\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}
, then for all
0\leqslant j<e
and for all
0\leqslant s<\alpha_{j+1}
,

\mathcal{C}_{b}([\,T^{(s)}_{j},(r,c)])=[\,T^{(s+1)}_{j},(r^{\prime},c-1)]
for some
r,c,r^{\prime}
.
We show that
\mathcal{V}_{b}(T^{(s+1)}_{j})=T^{(s)}_{j}
for all
0\leqslant j<e
and for all
0\leqslant s<\alpha_{j+1}
.
Following the notation in
Definition
3.2
, we first locate the rightmost column that contains nonzero arm excess, then determine
the topmost cell in row
\tilde{r}
in that column with nonzero arm excess. We denote by
a
the largest arm element in that cell.

By Lemma
3.26
, in
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
,
column
c-1
is the rightmost column with nonzero arm excess and

(r^{\prime},c-1)
is the topmost cell in column
c-1
with nonzero arm excess.

Case (1):

r^{\prime}=r
. In this case either cell
(r+1,c-1)
does not exist in
T^{(s)}_{j}
, or
\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r+1,c-1)>b
.

Case (1A):

\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)=\emptyset
.

m=\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)

and
b=\max(\mathsf{L}^{+}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c))
.
Since
r^{\prime}=r
,
q=m
,

T^{(s+1)}_{j}
is obtained by appending
m
to
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c-1)
, moving

\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)
into
\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c-1)
, and
removing cell
(r,c)
from
T^{(s)}_{j}
. Note that everything in

\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)
is greater than
m
and everything in

\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c-1)

is smaller or equal to
m
.

For the
\mathcal{V}_{b}
action, we have
a=m
and
b
is the greatest letter
in
\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c-1)
. Since every letter in

T^{(s+1)}_{j}(r^{\prime\prime},c)
is smaller than
m
for
r^{\prime\prime}<r
, we have

\tilde{r}=r
.

\mathcal{V}_{b}
acts on
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
by adding the cell
(r,c)
,
setting the hook entry to be
m
, and moving
(m,b]\cap\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c-1)
to
\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c)
. Then we recover
T^{(s)}_{j}
.

{{{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
--\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}b\\
\ast\\
m\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}_{b}}\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}b\\
\ast\\
-\\
--m\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}}}

{{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
--\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
b\\
\ast\\
q\,m\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}_{b}}{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}b\\
\ast\\
-\\
--q\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
m\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}}

Figure 9.
Left: Case (1A):
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)=\emptyset
. Right:
Case (1B):
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)\neq\emptyset
.

Case (1B):

\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)\neq\emptyset
.
m
is the only
element in
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)
,
q=\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)
and

b=\max\{x\in\mathsf{L}^{+}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}\mid x\leqslant m\}
.
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
is obtained
by appending
q
to
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c-1)
, setting

\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)
to be
m
, deleting
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}
, and moving
\{x\in\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s)}_{j}(r,c)}\mid q<x\leqslant m\}
to
\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c-1)
.

For the
\mathcal{V}_{b}
action,
a=q
and
b
is the greatest letter in

\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c-1)
. Since every letter in
T^{(s+1)}_{j}(r^{\prime\prime},c)

is smaller than
q
for
r^{\prime\prime}<r
and
m\geqslant q
,
\tilde{r}=r
.

\mathcal{V}_{b}
acts on
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
by setting
\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c)=q
,

\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c)=m
, and moving
(q,b]\cap\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c-1)

to
\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c)
. We recover
T^{(s)}_{j}
.

Case (2):

r^{\prime}\neq r
.

Case (2A):

\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)=\emptyset
. Note that in this
case,
\mathcal{C}_{b}
will move
m
somewhere else and remove
the cell
(r,c)
. Since
\mathsf{weight}(T^{(s+1)}_{j})=\mathsf{weight}(T^{(s)}_{j})
, we must have that
\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)=\emptyset
. So

b=q=m
.
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
is obtained from
T^{(s)}_{j}
by appending
m
to

\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r^{\prime},c-1)
and removing the cell
(r,c)
.

For the
\mathcal{V}_{b}
action,
a=m
. Since every letter in

T^{(s+1)}_{j}(r^{\prime\prime},c)
is smaller than
m
for
r^{\prime\prime}<r
, a new cell
(r,c)
is added,

\tilde{r}=r
.
\mathcal{V}_{b}
acts on
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
by moving
m
to

\mathsf{H}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c)
. We recover
T^{(s)}_{j}
.

{{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
--\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
-\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}m\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
--\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
-\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}_{b}}{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
--m\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\

\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\

-\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}}

{{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
--\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\

b\\
-\,m\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}_{b}}{\begin{array}[lb]{*{2}{|l}|}\cline{1-1}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
--b\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
--\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}-\\
m\\
-\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}}}

Figure 10.
Left: case (2A):
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)=\emptyset
. Right:
case (2B):
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)\neq\emptyset
.

Case (2B):

\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)\neq\emptyset
.
m
is the only
element in
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)
,

q=b=\max\{x\in\mathsf{L}^{+}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)\mid x\leqslant m\}
.
T^{(s+1)}_{j}

is obtained by appending
b
to
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r^{\prime},c-1)
,
replacing
b
in
\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)
with
m
, and removing
m
from

\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s)}_{j}}(r,c)
.

For the
\mathcal{V}_{b}
action,
a=b
.
Since every letter in
T^{(s+1)}_{j}(r^{\prime\prime},c)
is smaller than
b
for
r^{\prime\prime}<r
,
m

is the smallest letter
that is greater or equal to
b
in column
c
. Hence
\tilde{r}=r
.

\mathcal{V}_{b}
acts on
T^{(s+1)}_{j}
by removing
b
from

\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r^{\prime},c-1)
, replacing
m
in
\mathsf{L}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c)
with

b
, and attaching
m
to
\mathsf{A}_{T^{(s+1)}_{j}}(r,c)
. We recover
T^{(s)}_{j}
.

Therefore we have
\mathcal{V}_{b}(T^{(s+1)}_{j})=T^{(s)}_{j}
for all
0\leqslant j\leqslant e-1
, for all
0\leqslant s<\alpha_{j}
, and

\mathcal{V}(T^{(0)}_{j+1})=T^{(0)}_{j}
. It follows that we also recover the
recording tableau
F
. Thus
\mathcal{U}(T^{(0)}_{e})=(S,F)
.
∎

Corollary 3.32
.

The uncrowding map
\mathcal{U}
is a bijection between
\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)
and
\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}
with inverse
\mathcal{C}
.

3.6.
Alternative uncrowding on hook-valued tableaux

In Section
3.2
, we defined an uncrowding map sending hook-valued tableaux to pairs of tableaux with one being
set-valued and the other being column-flagged increasing. As hook-valued tableaux were introduced as a generalization of both set-valued
tableaux and multiset-valued tableaux, it is natural to ask if there is an uncrowding map taking hook-valued tableaux to pairs of tableaux
with one being multiset-valued. In this section we provide such a map.

Definition 3.33
.

The
multiset uncrowding bumping

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{b}\colon\mathsf{HVT}\rightarrow\mathsf{HVT}
is defined by the following algorithm:

(1)

Initialize
T
as the input.

(2)

If the leg excess of T equals zero, return T.

(3)

Find the topmost row that contains a cell with nonzero leg
excess. Within this column, find the cell with the largest value in its
leg. (This is the rightmost cell with nonzero leg
excess in the specified row.) Denote the row index and column index of
this cell by
r
and
c
, respectively. Denote the cell as
(r,c)
, its
largest leg entry by
\ell
, and its rightmost arm entry by
a
.

(4)

Look at the row above
(r,c)
(i.e. row
r+1
) and find the leftmost
number that is strictly greater than
\ell
.

•

If no such number exists, attach an empty cell to the end of row
r+1
and label the cell as
(r+1,\tilde{c})
,
where
\tilde{c}
is its column index. Let
k
be the empty character.

•

If such a number exists, label the value as
k
and the cell containing
k
as
(r+1,\tilde{c})
where
\tilde{c}
is the
cell’s column index.

We now break into cases:

(a)

If
\tilde{c}\not=c
, then remove

\ell
from
\mathsf{L}_{T}(r,c)
, replace
k
with
\ell
, and attach
k
to the
leg of
\mathsf{L}_{T}(r+1,\tilde{c})
.

(b)

If
\tilde{c}=c
then remove
[\ell,a]\cap\mathsf{A}_{T}(r,c)
from
\mathsf{A}_{T}(r,c)
where
[\ell,a]\cap\mathsf{A}_{T}(r,c)
is the multiset

\{z\in\mathsf{A}_{T}(r,c)\mid\ell\leqslant z\leqslant a\}
. Remove
\ell
from

\mathsf{L}_{T}(r,c)
, insert
[\ell,a]\cap\mathsf{A}_{T}(r,c)
into
\mathsf{A}_{T}(r+1,\tilde{c})
, replace the hook entry
of
(r+1,\tilde{c})
with
\ell
, and attach
k
to
\mathsf{L}_{T}(r+1,\tilde{c})
.

(5)

Output the resulting tableau.

Definition 3.34
.

The
multiset uncrowding insertion

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\colon\mathsf{HVT}\rightarrow\mathsf{HVT}
is defined as

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}(T)=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{b}^{d}(T)
, where the integer
d\geqslant 1
is minimal such that

\mathsf{shape}(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{b}^{d}(T))/\mathsf{shape}(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{b}^{d-1}(T))\neq\emptyset
or

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{b}^{d}(T)=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{b}^{d-1}(T)
.

Definition 3.35
.

Let
T\in\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)
with leg excess
\alpha
. The
multiset uncrowding map

\tilde{\mathcal{U}}\colon\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)\rightarrow\bigsqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{MVT}(\mu)\times\mathcal{F}(\mu/\lambda)

is defined by the following algorithm:

(1)

Let
\tilde{P}_{0}=T
and let
\tilde{Q}_{0}
be the flagged increasing tableau of shape

\lambda/\lambda
.

(2)

For
1\leqslant i\leqslant\alpha
, let
\tilde{P}_{i+1}=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}(\tilde{P}_{i})
. Let
r
be the index of the topmost
row of
\tilde{P}_{i}
containing a cell with nonzero leg excess and let
\tilde{r}
be the row index of the cell

\mathsf{shape}(\tilde{P}_{i+1})/\mathsf{shape}(\tilde{P}_{i})
. Then
\tilde{Q}_{i+1}
is obtained from
\tilde{Q}_{i}
by appending the cell

\mathsf{shape}(\tilde{P}_{i+1})/\mathsf{shape}(\tilde{P}_{i})
to
\tilde{Q}_{i}
and filling this cell with
\tilde{r}-r
.

Define
\tilde{\mathcal{U}}(T)=(\tilde{P}(T),\tilde{Q}(T)):=(\tilde{P}_{\alpha},\tilde{Q}_{\alpha})
.

\displaystyle\raisebox{28.45274pt}{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower \displaystyle=\tilde{Q}(T).

Proposition 3.37
.

Let
T\in\mathsf{HVT}
. Then
\tilde{\mathcal{U}}(T)
is well-defined.

Proof.

The statement follows from a similar argument to the proofs found in Corollary
3.7
and Lemma
3.9
.
∎

Similar to the uncrowding map
\mathcal{U}
, the multiset uncrowding map
\tilde{\mathcal{U}}
interwines with the corresponding
crystal operators.

Theorem 3.38
.

Let
T\in\mathsf{HVT}
.

(1)

If
f_{i}(T)=0
, then
f_{i}(\tilde{P}(T))=0
.

(2)

If
e_{i}(T)=0
, then
e_{i}(\tilde{P}(T))=0
.

(3)

If
f_{i}(T)\neq 0
, we have
f_{i}(\tilde{P}(T))=\tilde{P}(f_{i}(T))
and
\tilde{Q}(T)=\tilde{Q}(f_{i}(T))
.

(4)

If
e_{i}(T)\neq 0
, we have
e_{i}(\tilde{P}(T))=\tilde{P}(e_{i}(T))
and
\tilde{Q}(T)=\tilde{Q}(e_{i}(T))
.

Proof.

The proof follows similarly to those found in Proposition
3.12
, Lemma
3.13
,
and Theorem
3.14
.
∎

4.
Applications

In this section, we provide the expansion of the canonical Grothendieck polynomials
G_{\lambda}(x;\alpha,\beta)
in terms of
the stable symmetric Grothendieck polynomials
G_{\mu}(x;\beta=-1)
and in terms of the dual stable symmetric Grothendieck polynomials

g_{\mu}(x;\beta=1)
using techniques developed in
[
BM12
]
. We first review the basic definitions and Schur expansions
of the two polynomials.

Recall from (
1.1
), that the stable symmetric Grothendieck polynomial is the generating function of set-valued tableaux

G_{\mu}(x;-1)=\sum_{S\in\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)}(-1)^{|S|-|\mu|}x^{\mathsf{weight}(S)}.

Its Schur expansion can be obtained from the crystal structure on set-valued tableaux
[
MPS21
]

G_{\mu}(x;-1)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\\
e_{i}(S)=0\;\;\forall i\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|S|-|\mu|}\;s_{\mathsf{weight}(S)}.

Definition 4.1
.

The
reading word

\mathsf{word}(S)=w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{n}
of a set-valued tableau
S\in\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)
is obtained by reading the elements
in the rows of
S
from the top row to the bottom row in the following way. In each row, first ignore the smallest element of each cell
and read all remaining elements in descending order. Then read the smallest elements of each cell in ascending order.

Example 4.2
.

The reading word of
P(T)
in Example
3.6
is
\mathsf{word}(P(T))=8675423362111567
.

Example 4.3
.

The highest weight set-valued tableaux of shape
(2)
are

{\begin{array}[lb]{*{3}{|l}|}\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}\,,\quad\begin{array}[lb]{*{3}{|l}|}\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}2\\
1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}\,,\quad\begin{array}[lb]{*{3}{|l}|}\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}3\\
2\\
1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}\,,\quad\begin{array}[lb]{*{3}{|l}|}\cline{1-2}\cr\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}{{\hbox{$\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}4\\
3\\
2\\
1\end{array}$}}}\hfill\hbox{}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\end{array}\,,\quad\dots,}

which gives the Schur expansion

G_{(2)}(x;-1)=s_{2}-s_{21}+s_{211}-s_{2111}\pm\cdots.

The
dual stable symmetric Grothendieck polynomials

g_{\mu}(x;1)
are dual to
G_{\mu}(x;-1)
under the Hall inner product on the ring
of symmetric functions.

Definition 4.4
.

A
reverse plane partition
of shape
\mu
is a filling of the cells in the Ferrers diagram of
\mu
with positive integers, such that
the entries are weakly increasing in rows and columns. We denote the collection of all reverse plane partitions of shape
\mu
by

\mathsf{RPP}(\mu)
and the set of all reverse plane partitions by
\mathsf{RPP}
.

The
evaluation

\mathsf{ev}(R)
of a reverse plane partition
R\in\mathsf{RPP}
is a composition
\alpha=(\alpha_{i})_{i\geqslant 1}
,
where
\alpha_{i}
is the total number of columns in which
i
appears. The
reading word

\mathsf{word}(R)
is obtained by first circling
the bottommost occurrence of each letter in each column, and then reading the circled letters row-by-row from top to bottom and left
to right within each row.

Example 4.5
.

Consider the reverse plane partition

R=\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.40881pt\vbox{\kern 0.2044pt\hbox{\kern 0.40881pt\vbox to15.74207pt{\vss\hbox to15.33325pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.74207pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-15.74207pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt}&\lower 0.40881pt\vbox{\kern 0.2044pt\hbox{\kern 0.40881pt\vbox to15.74207pt{\vss\hbox to15.33325pt{\hss$2$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.74207pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-15.74207pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt}\cr\lower 0.40881pt\vbox{\kern 0.2044pt\hbox{\kern 0.40881pt\vbox to15.74207pt{\vss\hbox to15.33325pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.74207pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-15.74207pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt}&\lower 0.40881pt\vbox{\kern 0.2044pt\hbox{\kern 0.40881pt\vbox to15.74207pt{\vss\hbox to15.33325pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.74207pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-15.74207pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt}&\lower 0.40881pt\vbox{\kern 0.2044pt\hbox{\kern 0.40881pt\vbox to15.74207pt{\vss\hbox to15.33325pt{\hss$3$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.74207pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-15.74207pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}\,\in\mathsf{RPP}((3,2)).

\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.74207pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\vrule height=15.74207pt,width=0.40881pt}\kern-0.2044pt\kern-15.74207pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt\kern 15.33325pt\hrule width=16.15088pt,height=0.40881pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt},\;\mathsf{ev}(R)=(2,1,1),\;\mathsf{word}(R)=2113.

Lam and Pylyavskyy
[
LP07
]
showed that the dual stable symmetric Grothendieck polynomials
g_{\mu}(x;1)
are generating
functions of reverse plane partitions of shape
\mu

g_{\mu}(x;1)=\sum_{R\in\mathsf{RPP}(\mu)}x^{\mathsf{ev}(R)}.

They also provided the Schur expansion of the dual stable symmetric Grothendieck polynomials
[
LP07
, Theorem 9.8]

g_{\mu}(x;1)=\sum_{F}s_{\mathsf{innershape}(F)},

where the sum is over all flagged increasing tableaux whose outer shape is
\mu
.

Example 4.6
.

When
\mu=(\mu_{1})
is a partition with only one row, we have
g_{(\mu_{1})}(x;1)=s_{(\mu_{1})}
.

The flagged increasing tableaux of outer shape
(2,1,1)
are

,

1

,

2

,

2

1

.

Hence
g_{211}(x;1)=s_{211}+2s_{21}+s_{2}
.

According to
[
BM12
]
, a symmetric function
f_{\alpha}
over the ring
R
is said to have a
tableaux Schur expansion

if there is a set of tableaux
\mathbb{T}(\alpha)
and a weight function
\mathsf{wt}_{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{T}(\alpha)\to R
so that

f_{\alpha}=\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}(\alpha)}\mathsf{wt}_{\alpha}(T)s_{\mathsf{shape}(T)}.

Furthermore, any symmetric function with such a property has the following expansion in terms of
G_{\mu}(x;-1)
and
g_{\mu}(x;1)
.

Theorem 4.7
.

[
BM12
, Theorem 3.5]

Let
f_{\alpha}
be a symmetric function with a tableaux Schur expansion

f_{\alpha}=\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}(\alpha)}\mathsf{wt}_{\alpha}(T)s_{\mathsf{shape}(T)}
for some
\mathbb{T}(\alpha)
. Let

\mathbb{S}(\alpha)
and
\mathbb{R}(\alpha)
be defined as sets of set-valued tableaux and reverse plane partitions, respectively, by

\displaystyle S\in\mathbb{S}(\alpha)

\displaystyle\text{ if and only if }P(\mathsf{word}(S))\in\mathbb{T}(\alpha),\text{ and}

\displaystyle R\in\mathbb{R}(\alpha)

\displaystyle\text{ if and only if }P(\mathsf{word}(R))\in\mathbb{T}(\alpha),

where
P(w)
is the RSK insertion tableau of the word
w
.
We also extend
\mathsf{wt}_{\alpha}
to
\mathbb{S}(\alpha)
and
\mathbb{R}(\alpha)
by setting

\mathsf{wt}_{\alpha}(X):=\mathsf{wt}_{\alpha}(P(\mathsf{word}(X)))
for any
X\in\mathbb{S}(\alpha)
or
\mathbb{R}(\alpha)
. Then we have

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in\mathbb{R}(\alpha)}\mathsf{wt}_{\alpha}(R)G_{\mathsf{shape}(R)}(x;-1),\text{ and}

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}(\alpha)}\mathsf{wt}_{\alpha}(S)(-1)^{|S|-|\mathsf{shape}(S)|}g_{\mathsf{shape}(S)}(x;1).

Proposition 4.8
.

The canonical Grothendieck polynomials have a tableaux Schur expansion.

Proof.

Recall the uncrowding map on set-valued tableaux of Definition
3.1

\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{SVT}}:\,\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\longrightarrow\bigsqcup_{\nu\supseteq\mu}\mathsf{SSYT}(\nu)\times\mathcal{F}(\nu/\mu).

By Corollary
3.32
, we have a bijection

\mathcal{U}:\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)\to\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}=\bigsqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}(\mu).

Note that
\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}\subseteq\bigsqcup_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\times\mathcal{\hat{F}}(\mu/\lambda)
.
Denote

\phi_{\lambda}(S)=|\{F\in\hat{\mathcal{F}}\mid(S,F)\in\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}\}|.

Note that sometimes
\phi_{\lambda}(S)=0
.

Given
H\in\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda)
, we have
\mathcal{U}(H)=(S,F)\in\mathsf{SVT}(\mu)\times\mathcal{\hat{F}}(\mu/\lambda)
for some

\mu\supseteq\lambda
and
|\mu|=|\lambda|+a(H)
. We can also obtain
\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{SVT}}(S)=(T,Q)\in\mathsf{SSYT}(\nu)\times\mathcal{F}(\nu/\mu)
for some
\nu\supseteq\mu
and
|\nu|=|H|
. The weights of
H,S
and
T
are the same.
When
H
is highest weight, that is
e_{i}(H)=0
for all
i
, then
S
and
T
are also of highest weight and

\mathsf{weight}(H)=\mathsf{shape}(T)
. Denote by
\mathsf{HVT}_{h}(\lambda),\mathsf{SVT}_{h}(\lambda),\mathsf{SSYT}_{h}(\lambda)
the subset
of highest weight elements in
\mathsf{HVT}(\lambda),\mathsf{SVT}(\lambda),\mathsf{SSYT}(\lambda)
, respectively.

Applying
[
HS20
, Theorem 4.6]
and the above correspondence, we obtain

\displaystyle G_{\lambda}(x;\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{H\in\mathsf{HVT}_{h}(\lambda)}\alpha^{a(H)}\beta^{\ell(H)}s_{\mathsf{weight}(H)}=\sum_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\sum_{(S,F)\in\mathsf{K}_{\lambda}(\mu)}\alpha^{|\mu|-|\lambda|}\beta^{|S|-|\mu|}s_{\mathsf{weight}(S)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\sum_{S\in\mathsf{SVT}_{h}(\mu)}\phi_{\lambda}(S)\alpha^{|\mu|-|\lambda|}\beta^{|S|-|\mu|}s_{\mathsf{weight}(S)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\sum_{\nu\supseteq\mu}\sum_{T\in\mathsf{SSYT}_{h}(\nu)}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}(\nu/\mu)}\phi_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}^{-1}_{\mathsf{SVT}}(T,Q))\alpha^{|\mu|-|\lambda|}\beta^{|\nu|-|\mu|}s_{\mathsf{weight}(T)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\supseteq\lambda}\sum_{\nu\supseteq\mu}\sum_{T\in\mathsf{SSYT}_{h}(\nu)}\alpha^{|\mu|-|\lambda|}\beta^{|\nu|-|\mu|}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}(\nu/\mu)}\phi_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}^{-1}_{\mathsf{SVT}}(T,Q))s_{\mathsf{shape}(T)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}(\lambda)}\mathsf{wt}_{\lambda}(T)s_{\mathsf{shape}(T)},

where
\mathbb{T}(\lambda)=\{T\in\mathsf{SSYT}_{h}(\nu)\mid\nu\supseteq\lambda\}
and

\mathsf{wt}_{\lambda}(T)=\sum_{\mu:\lambda\subseteq\mu\subseteq\mathsf{shape}(T)}\alpha^{|\mu|-|\lambda|}\beta^{|\mathsf{shape}(T)|-|\mu|}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}(\mathsf{shape}(T)/\mu)}\phi_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}^{-1}_{\mathsf{SVT}}(T,Q)).

∎

Note that Proposition
4.8
in particular implies that the canonical Grothendieck polynomials are Schur positive.
This was known from
[
HS20
]
, but here an explicit tableaux formula is given.

Corollary 4.9
.

The canonical Grothendieck polynomials have
G_{\mu}(x;-1)
and
g_{\mu}(x;1)
expansions:

\displaystyle G_{\lambda}(x;\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in\mathbb{R}(\lambda)}\mathsf{wt}_{\lambda}(R)G_{\mathsf{shape}(R)}(x;-1),

\displaystyle G_{\lambda}(x;\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}(\lambda)}\mathsf{wt}_{\lambda}(S)(-1)^{|S|-|\mathsf{shape}(S)|}g_{\mathsf{shape}(S)}(x;1).
